Native-byte and fixed-width integer conversion
---------------------------------------------

PyLong_AsNativeBytes now validates operand access after validating its size and
endianness arguments, before reading integer data or writing converted bytes.
The check also covers PyLong_AsInt32/UInt32/Int64/UInt64, which delegate to this
path. Index conversion continues to validate acquired results through the
previously checked _PyNumber_Index implementation.

New tests cover foreign compact and multi-digit positive/negative values across
all four fixed-width APIs, size-only queries, truncation and wider native-byte
buffers, endian selection, unsigned-buffer and negative-rejection flags. On
access failure the byte buffer retains its sentinel contents; the existing
fixed-width wrappers also assert their output values retain their initial
sentinels. Permitted conversions are compared with exact-int controls. Native
index-result coverage now includes fixed-width conversions. A warning-handler
regression checks that ending debugger suspension or raising LookupError stops
conversion before the API writes output bytes.

access denial was expected: /tmp/pep805-nativebytes-before.log. The warning
lifetime test was added subsequently. The final focused run passes 154 tests:
/tmp/pep805-nativebytes-ft-focused.log.

Integer sign/export APIs, other native readers and buffer-view lifetimes remain

Integer sign queries and export
-------------------------------

PyLong_GetSign, PyLong_IsPositive, PyLong_IsNegative and PyLong_IsZero now
validate access before reading the integer sign. GetSign leaves its output
unchanged on failure; the test wrapper checks this with a sentinel.
PyLong_Export propagates failures from the checked scalar converter and clears
the export structure instead of asserting that conversion of an int cannot
fail. Its test wrapper initializes every output field and checks cleanup on
failure. Successful scalar and digit-array exports retain their existing
behavior.

Two added tests exercise all five APIs with foreign integer subclasses holding
zero, compact positive/negative values and positive/negative multi-digit values.
They require access denial outside debugger suspension and compare permitted
results with exact-int controls during suspension.

Before the fix, sign queries produced twenty failures:
/tmp/pep805-longsign-before.log. The export regression aborted an isolated test
worker at the obsolete conversion-success assertion:
/tmp/pep805-longexport-before.log. This exposed an integration failure from the
earlier checked scalar conversions. The post-fix focused run passes 83 tests:
/tmp/pep805-longexport-ft-focused.log.

UID/GID conversion error propagation
-----------------------------------

The numeric caller audit found that _Py_Uid_Converter and _Py_Gid_Converter
replaced every _PyNumber_Index failure with TypeError. They now replace only
TypeError, retaining the existing invalid-type diagnostic while propagating
IllegalThreadAccessException and other exceptions from index conversion.
This prevents pwd.getpwuid, grp.getgrgid and the shared POSIX converters from
masking access denial as a type mismatch.

Three new tests use read-only password/group database lookups to exercise both
converters. They cover a foreign integer subclass passed directly, an unchecked
foreign result from a native nb_index slot, and an exception raised by Python
__index__. Debugger-suspended positive controls compare with exact-int lookups,
including the possibility that the current UID/GID has no database entry. The
native-result test also verifies denial occurs before a subclass warning and
that the warning is issued for the permitted conversion. Invalid object inputs
continue to raise TypeError. No credential-changing call is used in these new
tests.

The pre-fix run of the three tests reported six errors:
/tmp/pep805-uidgid-before.log.

The preceding sign-caller audit found that slice-index and resource-limit
conversion call PyNumber_Index before reading the sign, pickling dispatches

operands before float comparison. Those paths therefore do not need speculative

audit. General C API/VM lifetime validation, unified runtime, shutdown, platform
coverage and performance remain unfinished.


Native slot-wrapper argument validation
---------------------------------------

The descriptor-entry audit reproduced a debug-build abort when a C extension
called float.__lt__ with a foreign integer operand. That route bypassed
PyObject_RichCompare's operand validation: float_richcompare ignored the failed
GetSign return, then returned a result with an exception set. The isolated
pre-fix worker aborted in _Py_CheckFunctionResult:
/tmp/pep805-slotwrapper-before.log.

wrapperdescr_raw_call now checks the receiver and uses _PyEval_CheckCallArgs to
validate the descriptor, argument containers, positional arguments and keyword
keys/values before dispatching to the slot wrapper. The common entry covers
unbound wrapper descriptors and bound method wrappers, including keyword-aware
slots. This prevents a retained bound wrapper from bypassing a protected
receiver's current access state. Call-protocol documentation describes this
boundary.

Four added tests cover all six float comparison wrappers, foreign int/float
operands, foreign float receivers, bound and unbound entry, protected list
receivers with Lock and RLock, and keyword arguments to dict.__init__. Permitted
calls run under debugger suspension or the protecting context. Denied keyword
calls must leave the target dictionary empty. The focused post-fix run passes
all four tests: /tmp/pep805-slotwrapper-ft-focused.log.

This closes the wrapper-descriptor route found after the preceding audit; it
does not establish safety for arbitrary C code invoking native slot pointers
directly. Other descriptor families, reentrant argument lifetimes and the wider
C API/VM audit remain unfinished, as do unified runtime, shutdown, platform
coverage and performance work.


Unbound native method-descriptor access
--------------------------------------

The next descriptor audit reproduced foreign integer payload reads through
int.bit_length, int.bit_count and int.as_integer_ratio invoked with
PyObject_Vectorcall. Unbound native method descriptors now validate their
receiver, positional arguments, keyword-name tuple, keyword names and keyword
values through method_check_args. The helper distinguishes keyword-capable
calling conventions so those entry points no longer discard kwnames during
validation. All seven native descriptor calling conventions use the helper,
including METH_METHOD. Ordinary wrong-arity and unsupported-keyword behavior
is retained for accessible arguments.

Four added tests exercise foreign receivers, positional values, keyword values
and keyword names, including debugger-suspended positive controls. Existing
native defining-class coverage now also invokes the unbound METH_METHOD
descriptor with an inaccessible keyword value that its implementation ignores;
access denial must leave the module counter unchanged. Permitted base and
derived instances still update the correct defining module's counter.

The initial three-test reproducer reported ten failures:
/tmp/pep805-methoddescr-before.log. Keyword-name and METH_METHOD coverage were
added afterwards. The call-protocol documentation records the new boundary.

Validation:
* All four builds succeeded:
  /tmp/pep805-methoddescr-{ft,gil,tier2,tracerefs}-build.log.
* All 49 added files passed --fail-env-changed in all four configurations:
  676 tests, with respectively two, eleven, eleven and ten skips:
  /tmp/pep805-methoddescr-{ft,gil,tier2,tracerefs}-added.log.
* test_number_access, test_native_python_call, test_descr, test_call, test_float
  and test_int passed in all configurations: 526 tests, six skips:
  /tmp/pep805-methoddescr-{ft,gil,tier2,tracerefs}-standard.log.
* test_number_access and test_native_python_call passed -R 3:3 in all
  configurations: 63 tests:
  /tmp/pep805-methoddescr-{ft,gil,tier2,tracerefs}-ref.log.

Bound native C functions, other descriptor families and argument access after
reentrant allocation/callbacks still need review. This does not complete the
C API/VM access audit, unified runtime, shutdown, platform coverage or
performance work.


Native C function and bound-method entry checks
----------------------------------------------

PyCFunction vectorcall entry now checks the callable, its retained non-module
receiver, positional arguments, keyword names and keyword values before native
execution. The tuple/dictionary calling convention uses the same receiver
check and _PyEval_CheckCallArgs. Module functions retain a native module state
context, rather than a bound receiver: access is governed by the callable's own
state, preserving explicitly synchronized module functions. Raw test-only C
function-pointer calls remain available to test individual API boundaries.

Three new tests cover foreign bound integer receivers and native positional and
keyword arguments across module functions, instance methods, class methods and
static methods. Keyword names are covered as well. Existing METH_METHOD tests
now cover both bound and unbound calls, including an ignored keyword value;
denial must occur before incrementing the defining module's counter. The first
three-test reproducer had thirty-nine failures:
/tmp/pep805-cfunction-before.log.

Integration exposed two required distinctions. Checking a retained module as a
normal receiver broke synchronized module functions; initial failed suite logs
are preserved at /tmp/pep805-cfunction-{ft,gil,tier2,tracerefs}-initial-added.log.
Those runs were stopped after the regression was identified. With that fixed,
PyImport_Import still passed internal frame globals through the native
__import__ call boundary, breaking sharing-state queries from other groups:
/tmp/pep805-cfunction-ft-import-failure-added.log. It now follows IMPORT_NAME's
default-importer fast path, keeping those globals inside the interpreter and
validating the module name. Custom import hooks retain the ordinary call path.
The NULL module-name contract is preserved with an explicit SystemError return;
the isolated failure before adding that guard is preserved at
/tmp/pep805-cfunction-ft-import-before-null-guard.log.

A new C API import test verifies custom-hook argument identity, level/fromlist,
result and exception propagation. Existing import tests cover the NULL input.
No existing tests were weakened. Call-protocol documentation records the new
native entry boundary and the module-context distinction.

Validation:
* All four final builds succeeded:
  /tmp/pep805-cfunction-{ft,gil,tier2,tracerefs}-build.log.
* All 49 added files passed --fail-env-changed in all four configurations:
  679 tests, with respectively two, eleven, eleven and ten skips:
  /tmp/pep805-cfunction-{ft,gil,tier2,tracerefs}-added.log.
  The FT/GIL runs preceded the final NULL-only import guard; the final standard
  and reference-leak runs below include that guard and its existing regression.
* Numeric/native-call, descriptor, call, float, int, C API import and importlib
  API suites passed on the final builds: 607 tests, six skips:
  /tmp/pep805-cfunction-{ft,gil,tier2,tracerefs}-standard.log.
* test_number_access, test_native_python_call and test_capi.test_import passed
  -R 3:3 on the final builds: 88 tests in all configurations:
  /tmp/pep805-cfunction-{ft,gil,tier2,tracerefs}-ref.log.

Native module state safety, other descriptor families, callback/allocation
reentrancy after entry validation and direct native-pointer calls remain to be
audited. C API/VM lifetime validation, unified runtime, shutdown, platform
coverage and performance work remain incomplete.


C function accessors and JSON encoder integration
--------------------------------------------------

PyCFunction_GetFunction, PyCFunction_GetFlags and PyCFunction_GetSelf now
validate the C function object before reading its fields. GetSelf also checks
the borrowed self object before returning it; a static function with no self
continues to return NULL without an exception. The unchecked GET macros remain
explicitly unchecked. This makes accessor failures visible to extension code
instead of exposing a foreign retained receiver.

The _json encoder constructor now handles a failed PyCFunction_GetFunction,
decrefs its partially initialized encoder and propagates the access exception.
The initial JSON reproducer aborted in _Py_CheckFunctionResult when the
exception was ignored: /tmp/pep805-cfunction-accessors-json-before.log.
Test-only wrappers cover accessor success, invalid-object contracts, foreign
function objects and a foreign native encoder. The focused accessor run passes
three tests in every configuration; the JSON case is included in the final
number-access run.

Validation:
* All four builds succeeded:
  /tmp/pep805-cfunction-accessors-{ft,gil,tier2,tracerefs}-build.log.
* The final 49-file suite passed in all four configurations: 683 tests, with
  respectively two, eleven, eleven and ten skips:
  /tmp/pep805-cfunction-final-{ft,gil,tier2,tracerefs}-added.log.
* The final numeric/native-call, descriptor, call, float, int, C API import and
  importlib API suites passed: 611 tests, six skips in every configuration:
  /tmp/pep805-cfunction-final-{ft,gil,tier2,tracerefs}-standard.log.
* test_number_access, test_native_python_call and test_capi.test_import passed
  with -R 3:3: 92 tests in every configuration:
  /tmp/pep805-cfunction-final-{ft,gil,tier2,tracerefs}-ref.log.

Other C APIs returning borrowed references, extension constructors that inspect
native callable fields, and reentrant callbacks during accessor validation still
need review. This remains an incremental reference implementation; unified
runtime, shutdown, platform coverage and performance work are unfinished.


Abstract object, sequence, mapping and iterator operand validation
-------------------------------------------------------------------

The public ``PyObject_Type``, ``PyObject_Size``, ``PyObject_LengthHint`` and
``PyMapping_Size`` entry points now validate their object operand before
reading the type header or dispatching through a length slot.
``PyObject_Length`` and ``PyMapping_Length`` inherit the same check. The
sequence size, concatenation, repetition, indexing, slicing, conversion and
search APIs validate their container operands; mapping key/value/item list
helpers validate their mapping; and iterator acquisition, next-item and send
paths validate their iterator. Buffer acquisition and object formatting now
also validate their primary object before reading type or buffer slots.
Limited-API and C-API test wrappers exercise foreign sequence and mapping
operands; failures occur before the foreign object is touched and the APIs
continue to work while the world is stopped.

Validation:
* All four builds succeeded while rebuilding ``Objects/abstract.c`` and the
  limited-API and C-API test modules:
  /tmp/pep805-abstractaudit-{ft,gil,tier2,tracerefs}-build.log.
* The focused foreign-operand test passed in every configuration:
  /tmp/pep805-abstractaudit-{ft,gil,tier2,tracerefs}-number.log.
* The sequence, iterator, number-access and native-call core tests passed in
  every configuration:
  /tmp/pep805-abstractaudit-{ft,gil,tier2,tracerefs}-core.log.
* The final 49-file suite passed in all four configurations: 685 tests, with
  respectively two, eleven, eleven and ten skips:
  /tmp/pep805-abstractaudit-{ft,gil,tier2,tracerefs}-added.log.
* The standard eight-file subset passed: 613 tests and six skips in every
  configuration:
  /tmp/pep805-abstractaudit-{ft,gil,tier2,tracerefs}-standard.log.
* The reference-leak run passed: 94 tests in every configuration:
  /tmp/pep805-abstractaudit-{ft,gil,tier2,tracerefs}-ref.log.

The remaining C API/VM audit still includes subscription key/value paths and
other borrowed-reference helpers, plus unified runtime, shutdown, platform
coverage and performance work.


List and tuple container API validation
---------------------------------------

The public list and tuple APIs now validate their container before reading the
type or item storage.  This covers size, borrowed and owned item access,
mutation, slicing, sorting, reversing and list-to-tuple conversion.  Stealing
APIs still release their item on an access failure, preserving their existing
ownership contract.  ``PySequence_Contains`` validates its sequence and item
while retaining the historical ``PySequence_Contains([], NULL)`` result.

A new sequence-access test invokes the APIs through raw native calls with
foreign list and tuple-subclass containers.  Each operation is rejected before
it can inspect the container; exact tuples remain shareable as required by the
primitive immutable rule.

Validation:
* All four builds succeeded after the list/tuple audit:
  /tmp/pep805-listtuple-{ft,gil,tier2,tracerefs}-build.log.
* The sequence, abstract C API, list and tuple tests passed in every
  configuration: 195 tests and one skip:
  /tmp/pep805-listtuple-{ft,gil,tier2,tracerefs}.log.

User-defined subscription containers still require a separate ownership/frame
audit before ``PyObject_GetItem`` can validate its container without changing
the existing callback behavior.  Borrowed dictionary iteration and other C API
helpers, unified runtime, shutdown, platform coverage and performance work
remain incomplete.


Set C API container and key validation
--------------------------------------

The public set C API now validates mutable set containers before accessing
their tables.  ``PySet_Contains``, ``PySet_Add`` and ``PySet_Discard`` also
validate their key before hashing or mutating the set, while ``PySet_Size``,
``PySet_Clear`` and ``PySet_Pop`` validate their receiver.  NULL handling and
the existing stolen-reference behavior of mutating APIs remain unchanged.

The new set-access test exercises all six entry points through raw native
calls with a foreign mutable set and confirms that every operation is denied
before it touches the set.  Exact frozensets remain immutable and shareable;
the existing C API suite continues to cover their special construction path.

Validation:
* All four builds succeeded after the set audit:
  /tmp/pep805-set-{ft,gil,tier2,tracerefs}-build.log.
* The focused set-access, limited C API set and standard set tests passed in
  every configuration: 663 tests:
  /tmp/pep805-set-{ft,gil,tier2,tracerefs}.log.


Bytes and bytearray C API operand validation
--------------------------------------------

The bytes and bytearray C APIs now validate object operands before reading
their layouts or exposing data pointers.  This covers size, string access,
representation, conversion, concatenation, resize and join entry points;
``PyBytes_Concat`` also validates both owned operands before mutating or
releasing its left-hand reference.  Exact bytes remain immutable and freely
shareable, while bytes subclasses and bytearrays retain local ownership.

The buffer API continues to delegate a NULL output view to the object’s
buffer slot, preserving the historical ``BufferError`` behavior for the
obsolete write-lock test.  A focused bytes-access test rejects foreign bytes
subclasses and bytearrays through raw native calls.

Validation:
* All four builds succeeded after the bytes audit:
  /tmp/pep805-bytes-{ft,gil,tier2,tracerefs}-build.log.
* The focused bytes-access test passed in all four configurations.
* The limited C API bytes and bytearray suites passed in all four
  configurations: 57 tests, with five skips in the bytes suite.


Capsule C API access validation
-------------------------------

PyCapsule_IsValid and the capsule accessor/mutator family now validate the
capsule object before inspecting its pointer, name, context or destructor.
Invalid capsules retain their ValueError contract, while an inaccessible
capsule reports the access exception without invoking capsule code.  The
limited-API test module exposes name and validity probes for a focused
foreign-capsule test.

Validation:
* All four builds succeeded after the capsule audit:
  /tmp/pep805-capsule-{ft,gil,tier2,tracerefs}-build.log.
* The focused capsule-access test and existing capsule import suite passed in
  all four configurations: 8 tests:
  /tmp/pep805-capsule-{ft,gil,tier2,tracerefs}.log.


Slice C API operand validation
------------------------------

``PySlice_Unpack`` and ``PySlice_GetIndices`` now validate the slice operand
before reading its fields.  NULL and non-slice arguments retain their
existing ``PyErr_BadInternalCall`` behavior, while a foreign slice (including
a slice subclass) is rejected with the PEP 805 access exception.  The
``PySlice_GetIndicesEx`` macro benefits through its existing delegation to
``PySlice_Unpack``.

The new slice-access test calls both entry points through raw native wrappers
from a worker thread and confirms that no foreign slice state is inspected.

Validation:
* All four builds succeeded after the slice audit:
  /tmp/pep805-slice-{ft,gil,tier2,tracerefs}-build.log.
* The focused slice-access and abstract C API tests passed in every
  configuration: 54 tests:
  /tmp/pep805-slice-{ft,gil,tier2,tracerefs}.log.


Complex C API operand validation
--------------------------------

``PyComplex_RealAsDouble``, ``PyComplex_ImagAsDouble`` and
``PyComplex_AsCComplex`` now validate their operand before checking the type
or reading the complex payload.  Foreign complex subclasses and objects that
would otherwise dispatch ``__complex__`` are rejected before user code or
object fields are accessed; exact complex values and the existing numeric
conversion behavior remain available.

The real, imaginary and C-complex conversion entry points are exercised by a
focused raw-call test with a foreign complex subclass.

Validation:
* All four builds succeeded after the complex audit:
  /tmp/pep805-complex-{ft,gil,tier2,tracerefs}-build.log.
* The focused complex-access test and existing complex C API suite passed in
  every configuration: 21 tests:
  /tmp/pep805-complex-{ft,gil,tier2,tracerefs}.log.


Memoryview and buffer-construction access validation
----------------------------------------------------

The public memoryview constructors now validate their object operands before
checking the concrete type, reading managed-buffer state or calling a buffer
exporter.  ``PyMemoryView_FromBuffer`` also validates a non-NULL borrowed
``Py_buffer.obj`` reference, and the internal buffer-proc constructor applies
the same check before invoking its callback.  NULL arguments retain a normal
``PyErr_BadArgument`` failure.

The focused test exercises ``PyMemoryView_FromObject`` and
``PyMemoryView_GetContiguous`` with a foreign mutable buffer subclass.  The
existing buffer conformance suite covers the constructor and release paths.

Validation:
* All four builds succeeded after the memoryview audit:
  /tmp/pep805-memoryview-{ft,gil,tier2,tracerefs}-build.log.
* The focused access test and buffer suite passed in every configuration:
  98 tests and two skips:
  /tmp/pep805-memoryview-{ft,gil,tier2,tracerefs}.log.


Dictionary C API receiver and key validation
---------------------------------------------

The principal public dictionary access and mutation APIs now validate their
dictionary and key/value operands before reading dictionary storage, hashing
keys or mutating entries.  This covers ``PyDict_GetItem`` and its error and
strong-reference variants, ``PyDict_SetItem``, ``PyDict_DelItem``,
``PyDict_Clear``, ``PyDict_Next``, ``PyDict_Keys``, ``PyDict_Values``,
``PyDict_Items``, ``PyDict_Size``, ``PyDict_Copy``, ``PyDict_Contains``,
``PyDict_SetDefault*`` and ``PyDict_Pop*``; the string-key wrappers inherit
the same checks.  Borrowed ``PyDict_Next``
iteration now fails at the foreign receiver before exposing key/value
references.  The limited-API clear wrapper propagates the error from the
void-returning C API so raw-call tests can observe it.

A focused dictionary-access test invokes all of these entry points with a
foreign mutable dict subclass and verifies that the dictionary is unchanged.

Validation:
* All four builds succeeded after the dictionary audit:
  /tmp/pep805-dict-{ft,gil,tier2,tracerefs}-build.log.
* The focused dictionary-access test and existing limited C API and dict
  suites passed in every configuration: 177 tests:
  /tmp/pep805-dict-{ft,gil,tier2,tracerefs}.log.

``PyDict_SetDefault*``, ``PyDict_Pop*`` and the dictionary merge/update entry
points now share the same receiver and operand checks.  Generic mapping
callbacks and reentrant mutation still receive the existing mutability checks
inside their operation loops.


Unicode C API operand validation
--------------------------------

The Unicode access boundaries now validate objects before reading their
representation.  The common Unicode type helper checks ownership before
type/layout inspection, and the public wide-character, UTF-8, length,
character read/write, substring, concatenation, search, join, copy and
encoded-object APIs apply the same rule.  Exact immutable strings remain
shareable; foreign string subclasses and mutable byte-buffer operands are
rejected before their storage or callbacks are touched.

The focused Unicode-access test invokes the conversion, scalar, search and
composition wrappers through raw native calls from a worker group.

Validation:
* All four builds succeeded after the Unicode audit:
  /tmp/pep805-unicode-{ft,gil,tier2,tracerefs}-build.log.
* The focused access test and existing limited C API Unicode suite passed in
  every configuration: 69 tests:
  /tmp/pep805-unicode-{ft,gil,tier2,tracerefs}.log.


Generic object hash and truth validation
----------------------------------------

``PyObject_Hash``, ``PyObject_HashNotImplemented`` and ``PyObject_IsTrue``
now validate their operand before reading its type slots or dispatching hash
and truth callbacks.  NULL arguments receive the existing internal-call error
contract, and an inaccessible object reports the PEP 805 access exception
without invoking user code.

Raw C API wrappers exercise hashing and truth testing with a foreign object.

Validation:
* All four builds succeeded after the generic object audit:
  /tmp/pep805-object-{ft,gil,tier2,tracerefs}-build.log.
* The focused object-access and abstract C API tests passed in every
  configuration: 54 tests:
  /tmp/pep805-object-{ft,gil,tier2,tracerefs}.log.
